2.11295pt}}}\coloneqq\boldsymbol{U}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{G}}
.

A symmetric tensor can be decomposed into a scalar part (i.e. proportional to the metric), a gradient part (i.e. the gradient of a vector) and a tensor part (a traceless-transverse tensor)
[
39
]
, called SVT decomposition. In general, the scalar part has spatial dependence. The existence of a Galilean vector implies that the expansion tensors (in the framework of Galilean structures) of any
\tau
-timelike vector have a spatially constant scalar mode as shown by relation (
101
). Another consequence is the form of the vorticity (
102
) which is expressed as function of the vector present in the gradient part of the expansion tensor. These relations were already shown to hold in
[
34
,
36
]
for Newton-Cartan and the NEN theory.

Finally, the definition (
98
) implies that the vector
\boldsymbol{B}
present in the Galilean connection in the NR limit [see equation (
20
)] is irrotational (
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{B}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Omega^{{\kern-10.30176pt{\boldsymbol{B}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-20.16277pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{B}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Omega^{{\kern-10.30176pt{\boldsymbol{B}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-20.16277pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{B}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Omega^{{\kern-6.85164pt{\boldsymbol{B}}\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-13.89522pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.5389pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{B}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Omega^{{\kern-5.84052pt{\boldsymbol{B}}\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-12.8841pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.52777pt}}}^{\alpha\beta}=0
). Therefore formulas (
101
) and (
102
) applied for
\boldsymbol{B}
give

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{B}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Theta^{{\kern-10.73232pt{\boldsymbol{B}}\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-20.59332pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.13892pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{B}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Theta^{{\kern-10.73232pt{\boldsymbol{B}}\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-20.59332pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.13892pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{B}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Theta^{{\kern-7.12386pt{\boldsymbol{B}}\kern 3.81111pt}}_{{\kern-14.16743pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.81111pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{B}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Theta^{{\kern-6.03497pt{\boldsymbol{B}}\kern 2.72223pt}}_{{\kern-13.07855pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.72223pt}}}^{\alpha\beta}=H(t)\hat{h}^{\alpha\beta}+\hat{D}^{\alpha}\hat{D}^{\beta}\sigma+\Xi^{\alpha\beta},

(103)

where
\sigma
is a scalar field and
B^{\mu}-\tilde{G}^{\mu}=\hat{D}^{\mu}\sigma
.

C.3
First order of the Lorentzian connection

From the relation of the difference between two connections, we have

\displaystyle\accentset{\lambda}{\Gamma}^{\gamma}_{\alpha\beta}-{\hat{\Gamma}}^{\gamma}_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=\accentset{\lambda}{g}^{\gamma\mu}\left(\hat{\nabla}_{(\alpha}\accentset{\lambda}{g}_{\beta)\mu}-\frac{1}{2}\hat{\nabla}_{\mu}\accentset{\lambda}{g}_{\alpha\beta}\right).

(104)

This leads to

\displaystyle\accentset{(1)}{\Gamma}^{\gamma}_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=\hat{h}^{\gamma\mu}\left(\hat{\nabla}_{(\alpha}\accentset{(1)}{g}_{\beta)\mu}-\frac{1}{2}\hat{\nabla}_{\mu}\accentset{(1)}{g}_{\alpha\beta}\right)+\accentset{(1)}{g}^{\,\gamma\mu}\left(\hat{\nabla}_{(\alpha}\accentset{(0)}{g}_{\beta)\mu}-\frac{1}{2}\hat{\nabla}_{\mu}\accentset{(0)}{g}_{\alpha\beta}\right).

(105)

This formula is in agreement with the “connection perturbation” introduced by
[
31
]
for the post-Newton-Cartan approximation (Table II in
[
31
]
).

Using
k^{\alpha\beta}=-\hat{h}^{\alpha\mu}\hat{h}^{\beta\nu}\accentset{(1)}{g}_{\mu\nu}
, which follows from (
15
), where
k^{\alpha\beta}
is defined with respect to the first order
\accentset{(1)}{g}^{\,\alpha\beta}
in equation (
17
), we have

\displaystyle\accentset{(1)}{\Gamma}^{\mu}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}^{\nu\alpha}

\displaystyle=\hat{D}^{\alpha}\left(\phi-\frac{1}{2}k^{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{B}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-8.11006pt{\boldsymbol{B}}\kern 3.51666pt}}_{{\kern-17.97107pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.51666pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{B}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-8.11006pt{\boldsymbol{B}}\kern 3.51666pt}}_{{\kern-17.97107pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.51666pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{B}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-5.41565pt{\boldsymbol{B}}\kern 2.1029pt}}_{{\kern-12.45923pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.1029pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{B}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-4.81482pt{\boldsymbol{B}}\kern 1.50208pt}}}_{\mu\nu}\right),

(106)

\displaystyle\accentset{(1)}{\Gamma}^{\gamma}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}^{\mu\alpha}\hat{h}^{\nu\beta}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\hat{D}^{\gamma}k^{\alpha\beta}-\hat{D}^{(\alpha}k^{\beta)\gamma}+B^{\gamma}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{B}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Theta^{{\kern-10.73232pt{\boldsymbol{B}}\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-20.59332pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-20.59332pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.72223pt}}}^{\alpha\beta}.

(107)

C.4
First order of the spacetime curvature tensor

Using the formula for the difference between two Riemann tensors

{\accentset{\lambda}{R}^{\alpha}}_{\beta\gamma\sigma}-{\hat{R}^{\alpha}}_{\beta\gamma\sigma}=2\hat{\nabla}_{[\gamma}\accentset{\lambda}{\Delta}^{\alpha}_{\sigma]\beta}+2\accentset{\lambda}{\Delta}^{\alpha}_{\mu[\gamma}\accentset{\lambda}{\Delta}^{\mu}_{\sigma]\beta},

(108)

where
\accentset{\lambda}{\Delta}^{\alpha}_{\beta\gamma}\coloneqq\accentset{\lambda}{\Gamma}^{\alpha}_{\beta\gamma}-\hat{\Gamma}^{\alpha}_{\beta\gamma}
, we obtain:

\displaystyle{\accentset{(1)}{R}^{\alpha}}_{\beta\gamma\sigma}

\displaystyle=2\hat{\nabla}_{[\gamma}\accentset{(1)}{\Gamma}^{\alpha}_{\sigma]\beta},

(109)

\displaystyle\accentset{(1)}{R}_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=2\hat{\nabla}_{[\mu}\accentset{(1)}{\Gamma}^{\mu}_{\alpha]\beta}.

(110)

Then,

\displaystyle\accentset{(1)}{R}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}^{\mu\alpha}\hat{h}^{\nu\beta}=\hat{\nabla}_{\gamma}\left(\hat{h}^{\alpha\mu}\hat{h}^{\beta\nu}\accentset{(1)}{\Gamma}^{\gamma}_{\mu\nu}\right)-\hat{h}^{\alpha\mu}\hat{\nabla}_{\nu}\left(\accentset{(1)}{\Gamma}^{\gamma}_{\gamma\nu}\hat{h}^{\nu\beta}\right).

(111)

Using equations (
106
) and (
107
) along with

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\left(\hat{D}_{c}\hat{D}^{c}k^{ij}+\hat{D}^{i}\hat{D}^{j}{k^{c}}_{c}\right)-\hat{D}_{c}\hat{D}^{(i}k^{j)c}-\hat{D}^{i}\hat{D}^{j}\phi,

(112)

\displaystyle\accentset{(1)}{R}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{\boldsymbol{B}}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{B}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\theta^{{\kern-8.4024pt{\boldsymbol{B}}\kern 1.64305pt}}}^{2}-\hat{D}_{i}\hat{D}^{i}\phi+\hat{D}_{j}\hat{D}^{j}{k^{i}}_{i}-\hat{D}_{i}\hat{D}_{j}k^{ij},

(113)

where we only keep spatial components as
\accentset{(1)}{R}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}^{\mu\alpha}\hat{h}^{\nu\beta}
is spatial, and
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{B}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\theta^{{\kern-8.4024pt{\boldsymbol{B}}\kern 2.72223pt}}_{{\kern-13.07855pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.72223pt}}}^{ij}\hat{h}_{ij}
.

Appendix D
Spatial metric at the limit and existence of the Galilean vector

D.1
A solution to equation (
56
)

While we have not yet found the general solution for
\hat{h}_{ij}
to the equation
\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{ij}=\bar{\mathcal{R}}_{ij}
, we give in this section a simple non-trivial solution. It can be found by writing the physical metric with a scalar-vector-tensor (SVT) decomposition with respect to the reference spatial metric
\bar{h}_{ij}
. In general, the contravariant and covariant components of the physical metric have a different decomposition:

\displaystyle\hat{h}_{ij}=A\bar{h}_{ij}+\bar{D}_{(i}A_{j)}+A_{ij},

(114)

\displaystyle\hat{h}^{ij}=B\bar{h}^{ij}+\bar{D}^{(i}B^{j)}+B^{ij},

(115)

where
A_{ij}
and
B^{ij}
are traceless-transverse tensors with respect to
\bar{h}_{ij}
, and
\bar{D}_{i}
is the covariant derivative with respect to that same metric, with
\bar{D}^{i}\coloneqq\bar{h}^{ci}\bar{D}_{c}
. We recall that
\hat{h}_{ij}
and
\hat{h}^{ij}
are by definition inverse from each-other, and the same for
\bar{h}_{ij}
and
\bar{h}^{ij}
.

We can rewrite equation (
56
) as

2\bar{D}_{[c}C^{c}_{j]i}+2C^{c}_{d[c}C^{d}_{j]i}=0,

(116)

with
C^{a}_{ij}\coloneqq\hat{h}^{ak}\left(\bar{D}_{(i}\hat{h}_{j)k}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{D}_{k}\hat{h}_{ij}\right)
. Then, a sufficient condition on
\hat{h}_{ij}
to be solution of the above equation is

\bar{D}_{k}\hat{h}_{ij}=0.

(117)

This condition implies that
\hat{h}_{ij}-\hat{h}_{kl}\bar{h}^{kl}\,\bar{h}_{ij}/3
is a traceless-transverse tensor with respect to
\bar{\boldsymbol{h}}
. Then, from the uniqueness of the SVT decomposition, the gradient mode (i.e.
\hat{D}_{(i}A_{j)}
) of
\hat{h}_{ij}
is zero and we have

\displaystyle\hat{h}_{ij}=A

\displaystyle\bar{h}_{ij}+A_{ij},

(118)

\displaystyle\bar{D}_{i}A=0\quad

\displaystyle;\quad\bar{D}_{k}A_{ij}=0.

(119)

The condition (
117
) implies
C^{a}_{ij}=0
, which is the difference between the physical
\hat{D}_{i}
and the reference
\bar{D}_{i}
connections. Therefore we have
\hat{D}_{i}=\bar{D}_{i}
, implying
\bar{D}_{k}\hat{h}^{ij}=0
. Then, the contravariant gradient mode also vanishes and we have

\displaystyle\hat{h}^{ij}=B

\displaystyle\bar{h}^{ij}+B^{ij},

(120)

\displaystyle\bar{D}_{i}B=0\quad

\displaystyle;\quad\bar{D}_{k}B^{ij}=0.

(121)

As for the homogeneity of
\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{ij}
, we have

\partial_{i}\hat{\mathcal{R}}=\partial_{i}\left(B^{cd}\bar{\mathcal{R}}_{cd}\right),

(122)

using
\partial_{i}(\bar{h}^{cd}\bar{\mathcal{R}}_{cd})=0
and equation (
56
). Therefore, for spherical or hyperbolic topologies, where by definition
\bar{\mathcal{R}}_{ij}=\pm 2\bar{h}_{ij}
, we obtain
\partial_{i}\hat{\mathcal{R}}=0
. In summary, from the guess (
117
), for
\tilde{\Sigma}=\mathbb{S}^{3}
or
\mathbb{H}^{3}
, the spatial scalar curvature of the Galilean structure is homogeneous. However, it is not necessarily isotropic as we have the following relation:

\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{ij}=\pm 2/A\left(\hat{h}_{ij}-A_{ij}\right),

(123)

with
\hat{h}_{ij}-\frac{1}{3}h^{cd}A_{cd}\,\hat{h}_{ij}\not=0
.

D.2
Expansion tensor of
G^{\mu}

We consider a vector
\boldsymbol{G}\in{\rm ker}(\bar{R}_{\mu\nu})
. Then, from relation (
48
) and the definition of
\bar{R}_{\mu\nu}
, in a coordinate system
\{\boldsymbol{\partial}_{t},\boldsymbol{\partial}x^{i}\}
adapted to the
\tau
-foliation with
\boldsymbol{G}=\boldsymbol{\partial}_{t}
, we can choose
\bar{h}_{ij}
such that

\partial_{t|_{G}}\bar{h}_{ij}=0.

(124)

From that equation and relation (
119
), the time derivative along
\boldsymbol{G}
of the physical metric
\hat{h}_{ij}
is

\partial_{t|_{G}}\hat{h}_{ij}=\frac{\partial_{t|_{G}}A}{A}\hat{h}_{ij}-\frac{\partial_{t|_{G}}A}{A}A_{ij}+\partial_{t|_{G}}A_{ij}.

(125)

From the definition of the expansion tensor (
97
), this leads to
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{G}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Theta^{{\kern-10.59502pt{\boldsymbol{G}}\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-20.15398pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.13892pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{G}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Theta^{{\kern-10.59502pt{\boldsymbol{G}}\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-20.15398pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.13892pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{G}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Theta^{{\kern-7.02579pt{\boldsymbol{G}}\kern 3.81111pt}}_{{\kern-13.8536pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.81111pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{G}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Theta^{{\kern-5.9369pt{\boldsymbol{G}}\kern 2.72223pt}}_{{\kern-12.76472pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.72223pt}}}_{ij}=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t|_{G}}\hat{h}_{ij}
. Then from (
125
) and the fact that
\hat{D}_{k}A=0
,
\hat{D}_{k}A_{ij}=0
and
\hat{D}_{k}\partial_{t|_{G}}A_{ij}=0
(using (
124
) along with the property
\hat{D}_{i}=\bar{D}_{i}
for the last relation), we have

\displaystyle\hat{D}_{k}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{G}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Theta^{{\kern-10.59502pt{\boldsymbol{G}}\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-20.15398pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.13892pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{G}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Theta^{{\kern-10.59502pt{\boldsymbol{G}}\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-20.15398pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.13892pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{G}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Theta^{{\kern-7.02579pt{\boldsymbol{G}}\kern 3.81111pt}}_{{\kern-13.8536pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.81111pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{G}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Theta^{{\kern-5.9369pt{\boldsymbol{G}}\kern 2.72223pt}}_{{\kern-12.76472pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.72223pt}}}_{ij}=0.

(126)

That property is equivalent as having (this follows from the unicity of the SVT decomposition)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{G}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Theta^{{\kern-10.59502pt{\boldsymbol{G}}\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-20.15398pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.13892pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{G}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Theta^{{\kern-10.59502pt{\boldsymbol{G}}\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-20.15398pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.13892pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{G}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Theta^{{\kern-7.02579pt{\boldsymbol{G}}\kern 3.81111pt}}_{{\kern-13.8536pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.81111pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{G}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Theta^{{\kern-5.9369pt{\boldsymbol{G}}\kern 2.72223pt}}_{{\kern-12.76472pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.72223pt}}}_{ij}=H\hat{h}_{ij}+\Xi_{ij},

(127)

with
\hat{D}_{k}H=0
and
D_{k}\Xi_{ij}=0
. From
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{G}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Theta^{{\kern-10.59502pt{\boldsymbol{G}}\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-20.15398pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.13892pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{G}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Theta^{{\kern-10.59502pt{\boldsymbol{G}}\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-20.15398pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.13892pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{G}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Theta^{{\kern-7.02579pt{\boldsymbol{G}}\kern 3.81111pt}}_{{\kern-13.8536pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.81111pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{G}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Theta^{{\kern-5.9369pt{\boldsymbol{G}}\kern 2.72223pt}}_{{\kern-12.76472pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.72223pt}}}_{ij}=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t|_{G}}\hat{h}_{ij}
and the definition of the volume of
\Sigma
, i.e.
V_{\Sigma}\coloneqq\int_{\Sigma}\sqrt{{\rm det}\,\hat{h}_{ij}}\,\mathrm{d}x^{3}
, we have
H(t)=\dot{V}_{\Sigma}/(3V_{\Sigma})
. Result (
127
) holds once condition (
117
) is fulfilled, or if we directly impose
\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{ij}=\hat{\mathcal{R}}(t)\,\hat{h}_{ij}/3
in the case
\tilde{\Sigma}=\mathbb{S}^{3}
or
\mathbb{H}^{3}
. Equation
127
is the first of the two conditions (
57
) and (
58
) for
\boldsymbol{G}
to be a Galilean vector.

From the general form of the physical spatial Ricci curvature (
123
) (depending on the guess (
117
)) and with (
118
), we see that imposing the physical spatial curvature to be only trace (i.e.
\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{ij}\propto\hat{h}_{ij}
) leads to
A_{ij}=0
, and
\partial_{t|_{G}}\hat{h}_{ij}\propto\bar{h}_{ij}\propto\hat{h}_{ij}
. Therefore, we have the following property:

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{ij}\propto\hat{h}_{ij}\Rightarrow\Xi_{ij}=0.

(128)

That is: an isotropic spatial curvature implies an isotropic expansion. It is however not yet clear whether or not the reverse property is true.

Remark 8
.

We expect that the general solution of equation (
56
) would rather constrain
\Xi_{ij}
to be only harmonic, i.e.
\hat{\Delta}\Xi_{ij}=0
, instead of gradient free. Indeed, being traceless-transverse and harmonic is the expected condition for a tensor representing global anisotropic expansion.

D.3
Vorticity of
G^{\mu}

From the Galilean limit, the vector
\boldsymbol{B}
present in the first order of
\accentset{\lambda}{g}^{\alpha\beta}
is irrotational with respect to the Galilean structure. Using relation (
100
) for the difference between two vorticity tensors, this implies that
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{G}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Omega^{{\kern-10.16446pt{\boldsymbol{G}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-19.72342pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{G}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Omega^{{\kern-10.16446pt{\boldsymbol{G}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-19.72342pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{G}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Omega^{{\kern-6.75357pt{\boldsymbol{G}}\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-13.58139pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.5389pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{G}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Omega^{{\kern-5.74245pt{\boldsymbol{G}}\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-12.57027pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.52777pt}}}^{ij}=\hat{D}^{[i}w^{j]}
where
\boldsymbol{w}=\boldsymbol{G}-\boldsymbol{B}
is a spatial vector. Then using
\hat{R}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}^{\mu\alpha}G^{\nu}=0
, which results from (
50
), we obtain

\hat{D}_{c}\hat{D}^{[i}w^{c]}-2\hat{D}^{i}H=0.

(129)

From the unicity of the Hodge decomposition, we have
\hat{D}_{c}\hat{D}^{[i}w^{c]}=0
, which implies
\hat{D}^{[i}w^{j]}=0
. We just showed that
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{G}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Omega^{{\kern-10.16446pt{\boldsymbol{G}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-19.72342pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{G}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Omega^{{\kern-10.16446pt{\boldsymbol{G}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-19.72342pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{G}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Omega^{{\kern-6.75357pt{\boldsymbol{G}}\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-13.58139pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.5389pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{G}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Omega^{{\kern-5.74245pt{\boldsymbol{G}}\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-12.57027pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.20712pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.10356pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.52777pt}}}^{ij}=0
.

In conclusion, the vector
\boldsymbol{G}
is a Galilean vector:
\boldsymbol{G}=\tilde{\boldsymbol{G}}
.

D.4
Uniqueness of the Galilean vector?

While
\boldsymbol{G}
is a Galilean vector, it is not uniquely defined if
\textrm{dim}[\ker(\bar{R}_{\alpha\beta})]>1
(see section
5.2.3
). For two independent Galilean vectors
\tilde{\boldsymbol{G}}_{(1)}
and
\tilde{\boldsymbol{G}}_{(2)}
, we define the spatial vector
{\boldsymbol{w}=\tilde{\boldsymbol{G}}_{(2)}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{G}}_{(1)}}
. Then from the difference between two expansion and vorticity tensors (equations (
99
) and (
100
)), the vector
\boldsymbol{v}
is constrained by:

\displaystyle\hat{D}_{i}w^{j}=0\quad;\quad w^{k}\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{ki}=0.

(130)

The second property is a direct consequence of the first one when calculating
D_{[i}D_{j]}w^{k}
. In conclusion, if there is not a unique inertial frame at the NR limit, then this frame is define up to a global translation in the direction of zero spatial curvature.

Remark 9
.

Even if there is not a unique inertial frame, the closedness of
\Sigma
still implies the existence of a “preferred” one, due to topological properties
[
32
,
27
]
. In other words, once we require the existence of a NR limit of either general relativity or the bi-connection theory, this implies the existence of a preferred observer in the Universe.
Question:
could the primordial power spectrum of the CMB be explained by the fondamental presence of such a preferred frame in the theory, and therefore be of topological origin?

Appendix E
Derivation of the dictionary

In this section, we consider isotropic expansion (
\Xi_{ij}=0
). Using the Taylor series (
11
) and (
12
) of the spacetime metric, in a coordinate system
\{\boldsymbol{\partial}_{t},\boldsymbol{\partial}x^{i}\}
adapted to the
\tau
-foliation and where
\boldsymbol{\partial}_{t}=\tilde{\boldsymbol{G}}
, we have

\displaystyle\accentset{\lambda}{g}^{\alpha\beta}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-\lambda+\lambda^{2}2\phi+\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda^{3}\right)&-\lambda\hat{D}^{i}\sigma+\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda^{2}\right)\\
-\lambda\hat{D}^{i}\sigma+\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda^{2}\right)&\hat{h}^{ij}+\lambda(k^{ij}-\hat{D}^{i}\sigma\hat{D}^{j}\sigma)+\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda^{2}\right)\end{array}\right)

and

\displaystyle\accentset{\lambda}{g}_{\alpha\beta}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-\frac{1}{\lambda}-2(\phi-\frac{1}{2}\hat{D}^{k}\sigma\hat{D}_{k}\sigma)+\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda\right)&-\hat{D}_{i}\sigma+\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda\right)\\
-\hat{D}_{i}\sigma+\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda\right)&\hat{h}_{ij}-\lambda k_{ij}+\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda^{2}\right)\end{array}\right),

where, as shown in Appendix
C.2
,
B^{\mu}-\tilde{G}^{\mu}\eqqcolon\hat{D}^{\mu}\sigma
with
\hat{D}_{i}\sigma\coloneqq\hat{h}_{ki}\hat{D}^{k}\sigma
. We also defined
k_{ij}\coloneqq k^{cd}\hat{h}_{ci}\hat{h}_{dj}
. We recall that
B^{\mu}
and
\phi
are defined in Section
3.4
where the NR limit is presented. For now,
\phi
is just a scalar field related to the 4-acceleration
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}a^{{\kern-8.41129pt{B}\kern 4.33765pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}a^{{\kern-8.41129pt{B}\kern of

, i.e.
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}a^{{\kern-8.41129pt{B}\kern The goal is to find if there is a relation between

,

and the gravitational field

, i.e. the 4-acceleration of inertial frames:

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\tilde{G}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}a^{{\kern-10.00433pt{\tilde{G}}\kern (135)

Using formula (60) in

]
, we can express
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}a^{{\kern-8.41129pt{B}\kern as function of

and obtain (we keep only spatial indices as all the tensors involved are spatial):

\displaystyle\hat{D}^{i}\phi-\hat{D}^{i}\Phi=\partial_{t|_{G}}\hat{D}^{i}\sigma+2\hat{D}_{k}\sigma\left(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Theta^{{\kern-10.21255pt{B}\kern 3.5389pt{k}{i}}}_{{\kern-12.70717pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.5389pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Omega^{{\kern-5.46925pt{B}\kern 2.52777pt{k}{i}}}_{{\kern-11.69604pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.52777pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\right)-\hat{D}^{k}\sigma\hat{D}_{k}\hat{D}^{i}\sigma.

(136)

Then using (
103
) and
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Omega^{{\kern-9.782pt{B}\kern 5.70836pt{k}{i}}}_{{\kern-18.49954pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.70836pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Omega^{{\kern-9.782pt{B}\kern 5.70836pt{k}{i}}}_{{\kern-18.49954pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.70836pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Omega^{{\kern-6.48038pt{B}\kern 3.5389pt{k}{i}}}_{{\kern-12.70717pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.5389pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{B}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Omega^{{\kern-5.46925pt{B}\kern 2.52777pt{k}{i}}}_{{\kern-11.69604pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.52777pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}=0
, we obtain
\hat{D}^{i}\left(\phi-\frac{1}{2}\hat{D}^{k}\sigma\hat{D}_{k}\sigma-\partial_{t|_{G}}\sigma\right)=\hat{D}^{i}\Phi
, which leads to

\displaystyle\phi-\frac{1}{2}\hat{D}^{k}\sigma\hat{D}_{k}\sigma-\partial_{t|_{G}}\sigma=\Phi,

(137)

up to an integration constant. We now have a link between
\phi
,
\sigma
and
\Phi
.

Let us consider the SVT decomposition of
k^{ij}-\hat{D}^{i}\sigma\hat{D}^{j}\sigma
, as is usually done in the weak field limit:

\displaystyle k^{ij}-\hat{D}^{i}\sigma\hat{D}^{j}\sigma\eqqcolon 2\psi\hat{h}^{ij}-2\hat{D}^{i}\hat{D}^{j}E-2\hat{D}^{(i}F^{j)}-2f^{ij},

(138)

with
\hat{D}_{k}F^{k}\coloneqq 0
and
f^{ij}\hat{h}_{ij}\coloneqq 0\eqqcolon\hat{D}_{k}f^{ki}
. The first order of the (modified) Einstein equation (
42
) is

\accentset{(1)}{R}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}^{\mu\alpha}\hat{h}^{\nu\beta}=\left(\frac{\kappa}{2}\rho+\Lambda\right)\hat{h}^{\alpha\beta}.

(139)

This holds either in the Euclidean case (general relativity), or in the non-Euclidean case (bi-connection theory). Using formula (
112
) for
\accentset{(1)}{R}_{\mu\nu}
, with (
138
) and (
137
) we obtain

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\kappa}{2}\rho+\Lambda\right)\hat{h}^{ij}

\displaystyle=\hat{h}^{ij}(\dot{H}+3H^{2})+\hat{h}^{ij}\left[\hat{\Delta}\psi+\hat{\Delta}(H\sigma)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\hat{D}^{i}\hat{D}^{j}\left[-\Phi+H\sigma+\psi+4KE\right]

(140)

\displaystyle\qquad+4K\hat{D}^{(i}F^{j)}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(6K-\hat{\Delta}\right)f^{ij},

where
K
is the spatial Gaussian curvature:
\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{ij}=2K/a^{2}\,\hat{h}_{ij}
. The unicity of the SVT decomposition applied on the above equation leads to

\displaystyle\frac{\kappa}{2}\rho+\Lambda=\dot{H}+3H^{2}+\hat{\Delta}\psi+\hat{\Delta}(H\sigma),

(141)

\displaystyle-\Phi+H\sigma+\psi+4KE=0,

(142)

\displaystyle 4K\hat{D}^{(i}F^{j)}=0,

(143)

\displaystyle\left(6K-\hat{\Delta}\right)f^{ij}=0.

(144)

So for any
K
, we have
f^{ij}=0
(more precisely,
f^{ij}
is harmonic), and using the Poisson equation (
80
) for
\Phi
with relation (
141
) we obtain

\displaystyle\psi+H\sigma=\Phi.

(145)

In the Euclidean case (i.e.
K=0
), which corresponds to the NR limit of Einstein’s equation and the usual Newton-to-GR dictionary,
E
and
F^{i}
are free. This is a usual gauge freedom in post-Newtonian theory. However, we see that in the non-Euclidean case, relations (
142
) and (
143
) imply
E=0
and
F^{i}=0
.

Finally, the spacetime metric can be written as follows as function of the gravitational potential:

\displaystyle\accentset{\lambda}{g}^{\alpha\beta}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-\lambda+2\lambda^{2}\left(\Phi+\frac{1}{2}\hat{D}^{k}\sigma\hat{D}_{k}\sigma+\partial_{t|_{G}}\sigma\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda^{3}\right)&-\lambda\hat{D}^{i}\sigma+\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda^{2}\right)\\
-\lambda\hat{D}^{i}\sigma+\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda^{2}\right)&\begin{array}[]{c}\hat{h}^{ij}+\lambda\left[2(\Phi-H\sigma)\hat{h}^{ij}\right.\\
\left.-2\hat{D}^{i}\hat{D}^{j}E-2\hat{D}^{(i}F^{j)}\right]+\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda^{2}\right)\end{array}\end{array}\right)

and

\displaystyle\accentset{\lambda}{g}_{\alpha\beta}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-\frac{1}{\lambda}-2\left(\Phi+\partial_{t|_{G}}\sigma\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda\right)&-\hat{D}_{i}\sigma+\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda\right)\\
-\hat{D}_{i}\sigma+\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda\right)&\begin{array}[]{c}\hat{h}_{ij}-\lambda\left[2(\Phi-H\sigma)\hat{h}_{ij}\right.\\
\left.\quad-2\hat{D}_{i}\hat{D}_{j}E-2\hat{D}_{(i}F_{j)}+\hat{D}_{i}\sigma\hat{D}_{j}\sigma\right]+\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda^{2}\right)\end{array}\end{array}\right),

where
E\not=0
and
F^{i}\not=0
only if
K=0
.

It seems that in the non-Euclidean case,
E
and
F^{i}
are not anymore gauge variables and are necessarily zero, i.e. the metric needs to be written with the same form as in the standard post-Newtonian gauge with Newton’s theory. In the next Appendix, we show that this peculiarity in the non-Euclidean case comes from a gauge fixing we implicitly made in Section
6
by assuming that
\bar{R}_{\mu\nu}
should not depend on
\lambda
.

Appendix F
Gauge freedom in the NR limit

F.1
Gauge freedom

Let us consider
\mathbb{R}^{4}
on which we define two Minkowski metrics
\eta_{\mu\nu}
and
\tilde{\eta}_{\mu\nu}
. The set of foliations which are flat with respect to
\eta_{\mu\nu}
are not necessarily flat with respect to
\tilde{\eta}_{\mu\nu}
. In other words, in a coordinate system where
\eta_{\mu\nu}={\rm diag}(-1;1;1;1)
, the relation
\tilde{\eta}_{\mu\nu}={\rm diag}(-1;1;1;1)
does not necessarily hold. This is because there are four degrees of freedom in defining
\tilde{\eta}_{\mu\nu}
with respect to
\eta_{\mu\nu}
, corresponding to a diffeomorphism
\varphi
of
\mathbb{R}^{4}
in itself. The freedom in changing
\tilde{\eta}_{\mu\nu}
while preserving its properties of being a Minkowski metric is related to the differential
\mathrm{d}\varphi:T\mathbb{R}^{4}\rightarrow T\mathbb{R}^{4}
of
\varphi
:

\displaystyle\tilde{\eta}_{\mu\nu}\overset{\rm freedom}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{d}\varphi\left(\tilde{\eta}_{\mu\nu}\right).

(152)

The same freedom applies for
R_{\mu\nu}
and
\bar{R}_{\mu\nu}
in the bi-connection theory. Therefore, the role of the bi-connection condition (
43
) is to constrain the four degrees of freedom in the definition of the physical connection with respect to the reference one (or inversely).

This freedom arising when two tensors are defined on the same manifold is also present for the family of Lorentzian metrics
\{\accentset{\lambda}{g}_{\mu\nu}\}_{\lambda>0}
used in the NR limit: there is a diffeomorphism freedom in defining
\accentset{{\lambda}}{g}_{\mu\nu}
with respect to
\accentset{{\lambda_{0}}}{g}_{\mu\nu}
, with
\lambda\not=\lambda_{0}
for any
\lambda_{0}>0
. However, while in the example related to the Minkowski metrics, this diffeomorphism is general, in the case of the family
\{\accentset{\lambda}{g}_{\mu\nu}\}_{\lambda>0}
, because we impose
\underset{\lambda\rightarrow\lambda_{0}}{\rm lim}\accentset{{\lambda}}{g}_{\mu\nu}=\accentset{{\lambda_{0}}}{g}_{\mu\nu}
, that diffeomorphism freedom is constrained to be the identity in the limit
\lambda\rightarrow\lambda_{0}
, and thus should also be parametrised by
\lambda
:
\underset{\lambda\rightarrow\lambda_{0}}{\rm lim}\accentset{\lambda}{\phi}={\rm Id}
.

A consequence is that, at first order in
(\lambda-\lambda_{0})
, the freedom in (re)defining
\accentset{{\lambda}}{g}_{\mu\nu}
with respect to
\accentset{{\lambda_{0}}}{g}_{\mu\nu}
is a Lie derivative (see
[
20
]
for a detailed explanation
9
9

9

Note that while
[
20
]
defines one manifold
\accentset{\lambda}{\mathcal{M}}
for each value of
\lambda
embedded in a 5-dimensional manifold
\mathcal{N}
(with a diffeomorphism
\accentset{\lambda}{\phi}
between
\accentset{{\lambda_{0}}}{\mathcal{M}}
and
\accentset{{\lambda}}{\mathcal{M}}
), this is equivalent as considering a single manifold
\mathcal{M}
with an auto-diffeomorphism, as is our case.
):

\displaystyle\accentset{{\lambda}}{g}_{\mu\nu}-\accentset{{\lambda_{0}}}{g}_{\mu\nu}\overset{\rm freedom}{\longrightarrow}\accentset{{\lambda}}{g}_{\mu\nu}-\accentset{{\lambda_{0}}}{g}_{\mu\nu}+(\lambda-\lambda_{0})\mathcal{L}_{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\,\accentset{{\lambda_{0}}}{g}_{\mu\nu}+\mathcal{O}\left((\lambda-\lambda_{0})^{2}\right),

(153)

where
\xi^{\mu}
is a vector field on
\mathcal{M}
. In perturbation theory, that freedom is called
a gauge freedom
as it does not change physics.

F.2
Gauge invariant variables in the NR limit

Under the gauge transformation describe in the previous section,
\accentset{(1)}{g}^{\,\mu\nu}
and
\accentset{(0)}{g}_{\mu\nu}
transform as

\displaystyle\accentset{(1)}{g}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle\ \accentset{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}{\longrightarrow}\ \accentset{(1)}{g}^{\,\mu\nu}+\mathcal{L}_{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\hat{h}^{\mu\nu},

(154)

\displaystyle\accentset{(0)}{g}_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle\ \accentset{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}{\longrightarrow}\ \accentset{(0)}{g}_{\mu\nu}+\mathcal{L}_{\boldsymbol{\xi}}(-\tau_{\mu}\tau_{\nu}).

(155)

The vector
\xi^{\mu}
can be written as

\displaystyle\xi^{\mu}=N\tilde{G}^{\mu}+\hat{D}^{\mu}\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{A}^{\mu},

(156)

where
\mathcal{A}^{\mu}\tau_{\mu}\coloneqq 0\eqqcolon\hat{D}_{\mu}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}
, and
N
and
\mathcal{A}
are scalar fields. We recall that
\tilde{G}^{\mu}
is a Galilean vector. Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\hat{h}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=-2NH\hat{h}^{\mu\nu}-2N\Xi^{\mu\nu}-2\hat{h}^{\alpha(\mu}\tilde{G}^{\nu)}\nabla_{\alpha}N-2\hat{D}^{\mu}\hat{D}^{\nu}\mathcal{A}-2\hat{D}^{(\mu}\mathcal{A}^{\nu)},

(157)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\boldsymbol{\xi}}(-\tau_{\mu}\tau_{\nu})

\displaystyle=-2\tau_{(\mu}\nabla_{\nu)}N,

(158)

where we used
\hat{h}^{\alpha\mu}\hat{\nabla}_{\alpha}\tilde{G}^{\nu}=-2H\hat{h}^{\mu\nu}-2\Xi^{\mu\nu}
. Then using the SVT decomposition introduced in Appendix
E
we have

\displaystyle\accentset{(1)}{g}^{\,\mu\nu}=-\tilde{G}^{\mu}\tilde{G}^{\nu}-2\tilde{G}^{(\mu}\hat{D}^{\nu)}\sigma+2\psi\hat{h}^{\mu\nu}-2\hat{D}^{\mu}\hat{D}^{\nu}E-2\hat{D}^{(\mu}F^{\nu)}-2f^{\mu\nu}.

(159)

Using the definition (
18
) of
\accentset{(0)}{g}_{\mu\nu}
along with formula (
92
) (in which we replace
\accentset{(0)}{u}^{\,\mu}
by
B^{\mu}\eqqcolon\tilde{G}^{\mu}+\hat{D}^{\mu}\sigma
; and we replace
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{B}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-8.11006pt{\boldsymbol{B}}\kern 3.51666pt}}_{{\kern-17.97107pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.51666pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{B}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-8.11006pt{\boldsymbol{B}}\kern 3.51666pt}}_{{\kern-17.97107pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.51666pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{B}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-5.41565pt{\boldsymbol{B}}\kern 2.1029pt}}_{{\kern-12.45923pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.1029pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{B}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-4.81482pt{\boldsymbol{B}}\kern 1.50208pt}}_{{\kern-11.8584pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.40326pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48228pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.20163pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.50208pt}}}_{\mu\nu}
by
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{\tilde{G}}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-9.18335pt{\boldsymbol{\tilde{G}}}\kern 3.51666pt}}_{{\kern-25.85007pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.51666pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{\tilde{G}}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-9.18335pt{\boldsymbol{\tilde{G}}}\kern 3.51666pt}}_{{\kern-25.85007pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.51666pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{\tilde{G}}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-7.76959pt{\boldsymbol{\tilde{G}}}\kern 2.1029pt}}_{{\kern-24.43631pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.1029pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{\tilde{G}}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-7.16876pt{\boldsymbol{\tilde{G}}}\kern 1.50208pt}}_{{\kern-23.83548pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.50208pt}}}_{\mu\nu}
), we get

\displaystyle\accentset{(0)}{g}_{\mu\nu}=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{\tilde{G}}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-9.18335pt{\boldsymbol{\tilde{G}}}\kern 3.51666pt}}_{{\kern-25.85007pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.51666pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{\tilde{G}}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-9.18335pt{\boldsymbol{\tilde{G}}}\kern 3.51666pt}}_{{\kern-25.85007pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.51666pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{\tilde{G}}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-7.76959pt{\boldsymbol{\tilde{G}}}\kern 2.1029pt}}_{{\kern-24.43631pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.1029pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{\tilde{G}}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-7.16876pt{\boldsymbol{\tilde{G}}}\kern 1.50208pt}}_{{\kern-23.83548pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.50208pt}}}_{\mu\nu}-2\tau_{(\mu}\hat{\nabla}_{\nu)}\sigma+\tau_{\mu}\tau_{\nu}\left(\hat{h}^{\alpha\beta}\hat{\nabla}_{\alpha}\sigma\hat{\nabla}_{\beta}\sigma-2\phi\right).

(160)

Then, from (
159
) and (
160
), the gauge transformations (
154
) and (
155
) induce a transformation of the variables
\sigma
,
\psi
,
E
,
F^{\mu}
,
f^{\mu\nu}
and
\phi
as follows:

\displaystyle\psi

\displaystyle\ \accentset{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}{\longrightarrow}\ \psi-HN,

(161)

\displaystyle\sigma

\displaystyle\ \accentset{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}{\longrightarrow}\ \sigma+N,

(162)

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle\ \accentset{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}{\longrightarrow}\ E+\mathcal{A},

(163)

\displaystyle F^{\mu}

\displaystyle\ \accentset{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}{\longrightarrow}\ F^{\mu}+\mathcal{A}^{\mu},

(164)

\displaystyle f^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle\ \accentset{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}{\longrightarrow}\ f^{\mu\nu}+N\Xi^{\mu\nu},

(165)

\displaystyle\phi+\frac{1}{2}\hat{h}^{\alpha\beta}\hat{\nabla}_{\alpha}\sigma\hat{\nabla}_{\beta}\sigma

\displaystyle\ \accentset{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}{\longrightarrow}\ \phi+\frac{1}{2}\hat{h}^{\alpha\beta}\hat{\nabla}_{\alpha}\sigma\hat{\nabla}_{\beta}\sigma+G^{\mu}\hat{\nabla}_{\mu}N.

(166)

Therefore, the following variables are gauge invariant:

\displaystyle\tilde{\Psi}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\psi+H\sigma,

(167)

\displaystyle\tilde{\Phi}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\phi+\frac{1}{2}\hat{h}^{\alpha\beta}\hat{\nabla}_{\alpha}\sigma\hat{\nabla}_{\beta}\sigma-G^{\mu}\hat{\nabla}_{\mu}\sigma,

(168)

\displaystyle\tilde{f}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle\coloneqq f^{\mu\nu}-\sigma\Xi^{\mu\nu}.

(169)

\tilde{\Psi}
and
\tilde{\Phi}
are the equivalent of the Bardeen potentials in the cosmological perturbation theory (weak field limit around a homogeneous and isotropic solution). We showed in Appendix
E
that
\tilde{\Psi}=\tilde{\Phi}
either in the Euclidean case (i.e. with Newton’s theory from Einstein’s equation, as expected), or in the non-Euclidean case (from the bi-connection theory). Therefore, the gravitational slip (i.e.
\tilde{\Psi}-\tilde{\Phi}
) is still zero in the bi-connection theory. Furthermore, we have
\tilde{\Psi}=\tilde{\Phi}=\Phi
, the gravitational potential, showing that this variable is gauge-invariant, again either in the Euclidean or non-Euclidean case. Equation (
162
)-(
164
) show that
\sigma
,
E
and
F^{\mu}
can be set to zero by a gauge transformation.

F.3
Gauge freedom in the reference curvature

As mentioned above, there is a diffeomorphism freedom in defining
\bar{R}_{\mu\nu}
with respect to
R_{\mu\nu}
. In the case of the NR limit, that freedom is present with respect to all the metrics
\accentset{\lambda}{g}_{\mu\nu}
and their curvatures. This implies that, once we consider a NR limit, the diffeomorphism freedom on defining
\bar{R}_{\mu\nu}
with respect to the family of Lorentzian metrics is parametrised by
\lambda
, leading, at first order, to

\displaystyle\bar{R}_{\mu\nu}=\accentset{(0)}{\bar{R}}_{\mu\nu}+\lambda\mathcal{L}_{X}\left(\accentset{(0)}{\bar{R}}_{\mu\nu}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda^{2}\right),

(170)

for any vector field
X^{\mu}
. Under a gauge transformation we have
X^{\mu}\ \accentset{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}{\longrightarrow}\ X^{\mu}+\lambda\xi^{\mu}
. As the difference between
\bar{R}_{\mu\nu}
and
\accentset{(0)}{\bar{R}}_{\mu\nu}
comes solely from diffeomorphism freedom, these two tensors have the same property, i.e. they take the form (
46
) in a certain (one for each) coordinate system
10
10

10

At first order, these coordinate systems differ by the transformation
x^{\mu}\ \accentset{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}{\longrightarrow}\ x^{\mu}+\lambda\xi^{\mu}
.
. In particular, as for
\bar{R}_{\mu\nu}
, there exists a vector
\accentset{(0)}{G}^{\mu}\in{\rm ker}(\accentset{(0)}{\bar{R}}_{\mu\nu})
such that
\mathcal{L}_{{\accentset{(0)}{\boldsymbol{G}}}}\accentset{(0)}{\bar{R}}_{\mu\nu}=0
.

Because until now we supposed that
\bar{R}_{\mu\nu}
was independent on
\lambda
, this means that the calculation of Section
6
and Appendix
E
was performed in a specific gauge where
X^{\mu}=0
. This is why we found in Appendix
E
that
E
and
F^{i}
needed to vanish. In what follows we explain why, without the gauge choice
X^{\mu}=0
,
E
and
F^{i}
do not vanish anymore.

In a general gauge, equation (
55
) takes the form

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mu\nu}\,\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{\accentset{(0)}{G}}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-10.828pt{\boldsymbol{\accentset{(0)}{G}}}\kern 3.51666pt}}_{{\kern-23.7883pt\mathchoice{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.51666pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{\accentset{(0)}{G}}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-10.828pt{\boldsymbol{\accentset{(0)}{G}}}\kern 3.51666pt}}_{{\kern-23.7883pt\mathchoice{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.51666pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{\accentset{(0)}{G}}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-9.41425pt{\boldsymbol{\accentset{(0)}{G}}}\kern 2.1029pt}}_{{\kern-16.61447pt\mathchoice{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.1029pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{\accentset{(0)}{G}}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-8.81342pt{\boldsymbol{\accentset{(0)}{G}}}\kern 1.50208pt}}_{{\kern-16.01364pt\mathchoice{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.50208pt}}}_{\alpha\mu}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{\accentset{(0)}{G}}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-10.828pt{\boldsymbol{\accentset{(0)}{G}}}\kern 3.51666pt}}_{{\kern-23.7883pt\mathchoice{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.51666pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{\accentset{(0)}{G}}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-10.828pt{\boldsymbol{\accentset{(0)}{G}}}\kern 3.51666pt}}_{{\kern-23.7883pt\mathchoice{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.51666pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{\accentset{(0)}{G}}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-9.41425pt{\boldsymbol{\accentset{(0)}{G}}}\kern 2.1029pt}}_{{\kern-16.61447pt\mathchoice{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.1029pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{\accentset{(0)}{G}}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-8.81342pt{\boldsymbol{\accentset{(0)}{G}}}\kern 1.50208pt}}_{{\kern-16.01364pt\mathchoice{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.20023pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.50208pt}}}_{\beta\nu}=\accentset{(0)}{\bar{R}}_{\mu\nu},

(171)

with
\accentset{(0)}{G}^{\mu}\,\accentset{(0)}{\bar{R}}_{\mu\nu}\coloneqq 0
, and the first order equation (
139
) (used to derive the dictionary) becomes

\displaystyle\left(\accentset{(1)}{R}_{\mu\nu}-\mathcal{L}_{X}\left(\accentset{(0)}{\bar{R}}_{\mu\nu}\right)\right)\hat{h}^{\mu\alpha}\hat{h}^{\nu\beta}=\left(\frac{\kappa}{2}\rho+\Lambda\right)\hat{h}^{\alpha\beta}.

(172)

Because
\accentset{(0)}{\bar{R}}_{\mu\nu}
has the same properties as
{\bar{R}}_{\mu\nu}
, the resolution of equation (
171
) is equivalent as solving equation (
55
) in Section
6.2.1
. In particular,
\accentset{(0)}{G}^{\mu}
corresponds to a Galilean vector, and in Euclidean, spherical or hyperbolic topologies without anisotropic expansion we have

\displaystyle\accentset{(0)}{\bar{R}}_{\mu\nu}=2K\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{\tilde{G}}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-9.18335pt{\boldsymbol{\tilde{G}}}\kern 3.51666pt}}_{{\kern-25.85007pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.51666pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{\tilde{G}}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-9.18335pt{\boldsymbol{\tilde{G}}}\kern 3.51666pt}}_{{\kern-25.85007pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.51666pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{\tilde{G}}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-7.76959pt{\boldsymbol{\tilde{G}}}\kern 2.1029pt}}_{{\kern-24.43631pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.1029pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\boldsymbol{\tilde{G}}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-7.16876pt{\boldsymbol{\tilde{G}}}\kern 1.50208pt}}_{{\kern-23.83548pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.50208pt}}}_{\mu\nu}.

(173)

Then, writing
X^{\mu}\eqqcolon M\tilde{G}^{\mu}+\hat{D}^{\mu}\mathcal{X}+\mathcal{X}^{\mu}
with
\mathcal{X}^{\mu}\tau_{\mu}\coloneqq 0\eqqcolon\hat{D}_{\mu}\mathcal{X}^{\mu}
, the additional term in equation (
172
), compared to equation (
139
), takes the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{X}\left(\accentset{(0)}{\bar{R}}_{\mu\nu}\right)\hat{h}^{\mu\alpha}\hat{h}^{\nu\beta}=4K\left(\hat{D}^{\mu}\hat{D}^{\nu}\mathcal{X}+2\hat{D}^{(\mu}\mathcal{X}^{\nu)}\right).

(174)

A consequence is that reperforming the calculation of Section
E
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\kappa}{2}\rho+\Lambda\right)\hat{h}^{ij}

\displaystyle=\hat{h}^{ij}(\dot{H}+3H^{2})+\hat{h}^{ij}\left[\hat{\Delta}\psi+\hat{\Delta}(H\sigma)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\hat{D}^{i}\hat{D}^{j}\left[-\Phi+H\sigma+\psi+4K(E+\mathcal{X})\right]

(175)

\displaystyle\qquad+4K\hat{D}^{(i}\left(F^{j)}+\mathcal{X}^{j)}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(6K-\hat{\Delta}\right)f^{ij}.

Therefore,
E
and
F^{i}
are not anymore imposed to be zero, instead the gauge-invariant variables
\mathcal{C}\coloneqq E+\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{C}^{i}\coloneqq F^{i}+\mathcal{X}^{i}
are zero.

In conclusion, from either the Einstein equation (Euclidean case) or the bi-connection theory (non-Euclidean case), the spacetime metric
\accentset{\lambda}{g}^{\mu\nu}
(respectively
\accentset{\lambda}{g}_{\mu\nu}
) takes the form (
6.3
) [respectively (
6.3
)] as function of the gravitational potential, with
\sigma
,
E
and
F^{i}
being free gauge fields in both cases.
